# Changelog — Authoritative Action Ledger

The cumulative, append-only record of **actions taken** in this repository — across backlog
items, repository issues, and ad-hoc work. It is the authoritative answer to *"what was done
here, ever?"* Every session records its actions here at close-out (`SESSION_RUNNER.md`
Phase 3F); Phase 0 reconciles it against `git log` and backfills anything a crashed or
out-of-band session missed. Taking an action — any commit, or any non-commit action — and
not recording it is failure mode #27. Old entries may be archived, never deleted.

**The rules** — how to add an entry, source tags, reading and archiving — are in
[§The Action Ledger](docs/methodology/FRAMEWORK_APPARATUS.md#the-action-ledger), which `bin/sync`
keeps current. ledger-format: 2 — keep this marker; `bin/status` reads it.

**Archived 230 record(s), 2026-06-30 → 2026-08-24** into [`docs/archive/CHANGELOG-through-2026-08-24.md`](docs/archive/CHANGELOG-through-2026-08-24.md) — same format, same order, frozen.
Losslessness is proved by [`docs/archive/CHANGELOG-through-2026-08-24.md.verify.sh`](docs/archive/CHANGELOG-through-2026-08-24.md.verify.sh), which re-derives L1/L2/L3 from git; run it rather
than trusting this sentence. Written by `methodology_trim.py` v1.1.1.

## 2026-10

### 2026-10-28 · [BL-Eight more regexes and one paragraph reader that are quadratic on a long line or a long paragraph, none of them in Session 318's diff] Session 334 review round: the four cures agree with the old spelling on about 400 million strings and 3 million documents, the answers were pinned only on strings of 260 characters or fewer and 137 new mutants say where (106 killed by the rows added, 31 argued), 7 shapes filed, 5 numbers in commit messages corrected

`wf_4c0e316d-989`: five read-only lenses over `08b271f..5ebc915` (equivalence, complexity, claims, siblings, mutation), each finding re-run by two refuting verifiers (73 agents, 8.2 million subagent tokens, 2,085 tool calls, 3 hours 34 minutes, machine load 5 to 250 from other projects and from the workflow itself, so every absolute time in the agents' reports is an upper bound and the session re-ran what it cites). 34 findings: 28 held (many narrower than claimed), 2 split between the verifiers, 2 refuted by both (the comment about "lists" in the knitr oracle file, which is right when a list is the argument list of an `options(` call; and the mutation lens's claim that 27 survivors were all equivalent, which was false for five and is how the real findings below were found).

**Equivalence (1 finding, benign, behaviour change)**: the old `htmlLineHasText` threw `RangeError` (the regex keeps a backtrack entry per character of a tag body) on a tag body of about 9 million characters or more (Node 22.23.3: 8,000,000 answered, 9,000,000 threw, checked here); the cure answers at any length. Pinned: `"<a " + "x".repeat(10_000_000) + ">"` is read. About 400 million old-versus-new comparisons at function level (exhaustive short strings over wide alphabets, every BMP code unit in templates, grammar-based and random generators) and about 3 million random documents through `findHeadings` and `buildOutline` (five engine hints, both render modes; 1.25 million spliced into corpus documents, and all 88,015 corpus documents under five engine and mode combinations) found no other difference; 12 planted broken variants were caught by the harness except the 4 that are equivalent by construction.

**Mutation (15 findings, the weight of the round)**: the cured functions' ANSWERS were pinned only on strings of at most about 260 characters over small alphabets, and the document rows pin time, not answers (a cured function replaced by a constant kept all 32 rows green; the comment said otherwise and is reworded). The lens invented 184 edits; 127 survived the tests as committed, 82 of them real gaps: caps of 4,096, 65,536 or 2^20 on a scan, a tag body, a quoted string, a name, a blank run or the number of names; a guard that looks at part of the value (all but the last character, the first or last 4,096 or 2^20) and so brings the quadratic back while keeping every answer; every whitespace character no alphabet held (vertical tab, form feed, U+1680, U+2000 to U+200A, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000, BOM); a backtick or curly quote taken for a quote; `$`, an accented letter, an arrow, `&a` and `!a`; and the call sites (`knitrCodeMasksPrinters`' allowed names, a call that does not close, `x.options(`; both YAML alias sites on a nested anchor, a no-break space and a 5,000-character name; both `htmlLineHasText` callers; the call in `linkDefinitionAt`). **One was a pre-existing cost guard nobody pinned**: deleting `at = close;` after an `options(` call keeps every answer and makes n copies of `options(` and one `)` cost n squared (10,000: 3.7 s against 12 ms; Session 309's row has 40,000 UNCLOSED calls, which return at the first). Rows added (`990cade`, `85cce1f`, `761f308`, `221e12b`, `1a2b3a0`): long-line and every-whitespace rows asked of the old function too, the guard's terminator last or beyond a megabyte, 12 call-site rows for the knitr table, 5 document rows for the YAML sites, 2 for the htmlLineHasText callers, a document row for the 4,096-character cap of the table-header question (a tag-only line of 4,203 characters cuts a tag in half), clock rows for a blank or tab run after a name, 60,000 names, the closed `options(` run, and a native scan after every tag, brace or hash (the lens had reported four of those linear because its `indexOf(...) === -2` let the compiler drop the call; consumed by a comparison the compiler cannot fold they cost 3.5 s (400,000 tags) and about 2.8 s (500,000 braces or `#`; measured at 100,000 to 800,000, interpolated) and the 72,000-character rows pass them), named sizes with a row that pins them. **137 mutants** in `tools/mutation-run/mutants/s334-review-{functions,wiring,tests}.json`: 106 killed by the rows added after the review, 31 survive on purpose (14 equivalent edits re-built by two verifiers over 1.2M to 62M strings each; 7 equivalent at the callers or in the data; 2 with NO witness, found by neither the lens nor 160,000 more random documents and left unpinned, HC04 and HC05; 3 reachable only by an invalid YAML document, an undefined alias being a parse error to quarto; the dead `max.print` entry; 4 test-side edits that cannot fail a test). Totals for the session: 232 edits in nine lists, 192 killed, 40 survive on purpose.

**A claim that was wrong, caught by rendering**: the first round's fourth link-definition row said `[a]: /u{#id} x` (attributes touching the URL) is swallowed, quarto's answer, as the sibling rows are. It was not measured. Nine documents rendered with quarto 1.7.33 (`--to html --no-execute`) say: junk after attributes that follow a blank is refused (swallowed) at 15, 17, 5,000 and 70,000 characters of URL, the same without junk is accepted, and attributes that TOUCH the URL (`u{#id} x`, `/u{#id} x`, 5,000 letters and `{#id} x`) are ACCEPTED by quarto and refused here. The row is gone; three RESIDUAL rows pin this build's answer with quarto's in the comment; filed. (Learning 650 applies to `qmd-stacked-enders.test.ts`: a row's claim is quarto's answer by construction. The rows with the definition LAST before the opener are the ones that exercise the call; every row added first put a thematic break last, which decides alone, so none of the 4 link-wiring survivors died.)

**Sibling shapes (4 findings, all pre-existing, 3 new)**: filed in `BACKLOG.md` ("Three more slow shapes found by Session 334's review round" and the amended `isAttributeBlock` and Session 319 items): a SECOND quadratic regex (`FENCE_RUN`, `refs.ts:630`) on the path of the recorded `isAttributeBlock` item, so that item's guessed cure leaves the blank shape quadratic (176 / 670 / 2,615 / 10,479 ms at n = 8,000 to 64,000; both tests replaced 9 / 13 / 17 / 27 ms); `liveDiv` and the skip-ended loops walking every ended quote-hosted `:::` opener on every later line (5.4 s at 32,000 openers and headings); `rangeWithinCell` and `rangeInSameLangBody` calling an O(document) `embeddedCellAt` per line (3.7 to 4.3 s at 2,000 cells and a 2,000-line cell); `endMarkerBelow` (linear, 33 microseconds a line); and `KNITR_MASK_ASSIGN` alternations 4 and 6 on `class(`, `attr(`, `attributes(` and `f(print)` (already recorded for two of the shapes; 19.8 s at 400,000 characters). Two answers where this build and quarto differ, filed as one item: the touching attributes above and the dead `max.print` entry.

**Complexity (4 findings, decided against)**: the cures cost a constant factor on some inputs, measured at function level and gone at document level (no document shape above 1.13x; 21 shapes, 7 interleaved repetitions, load 17 to 60). `htmlLineHasText` tests each non-`<` character with `/\S/` (about 16 to 20 ns a character), so a tag line padded with blanks is 2 to 20x slower than the native `replace` plus `trim` (1,000,000 blanks after `<a>`: 28 ms before, 68 to 95 ms now, linear) while short tag lines are 0.34 to 0.57x (faster); `knitrOptionNames` is 1.24 to 1.37x on an option list that names many options and 3 to 20x on a megabyte argument list that names nothing (the lookbehind is tried at every position the old pattern skipped, about 12 ns a character, linear); `yamlAliasName` costs 10 to 20 ns more on a short value and 4 to 7x on a 200-character non-alias value (the terminator pre-scan); `linkAttributesHaveJunk` is faster on ordinary URLs and about 2x slower on the rare accepted-junk shape. A sticky `/\s*/y` or a char-code fast path would remove the first; it was not taken, for tens of milliseconds on a megabyte-class blank run in a shape no document has, and a change to code that 300 million strings just agreed with. The same decision as Session 327's.

**Claims (10 findings, wording)**: five numbers in commit messages are wrong and the commits are not amended, so this is the correction: the enumeration sizes quoted in `855e0e0`, `e3545a8`, `11102a0`, `50fa22b`, `5917d42`, `ae72f74`, `6c1417e` and `b48ecf7` are partly the pinned TRUE or name counts and partly only the longest level (the sizes are the ones above; `b48ecf7`'s 14.3 million is the html file's total, the yaml file enumerates 16.3 million); `11102a0` says 32 rows fail with the seam missing, it is 33 of 34; `ae72f74` says shapes the parent read linearly are within 5% of it, which is false for the 200,000-digit `options(` control (22 to 46% slower, about 2 ms) and holds for assignment lists; `0c072f6` says "plus 7 argued equivalent", the first run left 13 alive, 4 expected killed and 9 argued equivalent; `5ebc915` stands. Comments and two mutant witnesses (`H25`'s `<a>"`, `L02`'s `x\t{a}b`, which gave the same answer under the mutant) were corrected in `5678884`; the `knitrOptionNames` comment no longer reads as a rule about the old pattern.

### 2026-10-28 · [BL-Eight more regexes and one paragraph reader that are quadratic on a long line or a long paragraph, none of them in Session 318's diff] Session 334: parts (4) to (7) closed -- `htmlLineHasText`, `linkAttributesHaveJunk`, `knitrOptionNames` and `yamlAliasName` read their text once, and the item is complete (g02, eighteenth session)

All four are in `src/core/qmd/model.ts`; each was a pattern that fails only at the END of the text it reads, so every start (or every give-back of a greedy part) read on to that end. Each is a function now, exported for its oracle file, with the old spelling copied into the test as the oracle (Learning 738) beside a reading by definition that uses no regex. **Part (4)** `htmlLineHasText` (`855e0e0` seam and oracle, `e3545a8` cure): one left-to-right scan that answers at the first character that is neither whitespace nor inside a tag, because a `<` that opens no tag already means text remains (the global `replace` went on trying every later `<`); the new helper `htmlTagBodyEnd` finds a tag's `>`. 40,000 `<` after a tag took 3.9 s, 17 ms now, 1,000,000 `<` 34 ms; 94.5% of the time was in the regex (`node --cpu-prof`, 20,000 units, load 8 to 10). The answer is observable: a tag-only line is no table header, so `x y` over a dash rule becomes a setext heading. **Part (5)** `linkAttributesHaveJunk` (`11102a0`, `50fa22b`): the first `{` at index 1 or later inside the leading run of non-blanks, or right after the blanks that follow it, decides for every later one (a later `{` before the `}` shares it; one after it needs a non-blank right after the `}`), then `indexOf("}")`. 60,000 braces 3.3 s, 1,000,000 braces 12 ms; profile 89.2%. **Part (6)** `knitrOptionNames` (`5917d42`, `ae72f74`): `/(?<![\w.])[\w.]+(?=[ \t]*=)/g`, the name being the run without its leading digits and underscores; 72,000 letters 3.2 s, 1,000,000 letters 99 ms; profile 83.1%. **Part (7)** `yamlAliasName` (`6c1417e`, `b48ecf7`): a value that holds a line terminator (LF, CR, U+2028, U+2029) can never match, because every part of the pattern excludes all four, so the guard answers null first and the pattern runs on the rest, where it is linear (its only quadratic shape was a `#` run before a terminator); 72,000 `#`, a CR and a letter about 3 s, 7 ms now; profile 84.6%. **The item's own cure for (7) was needless** (a second pattern for the terminator case) **and the lookahead idiom of the fence runs is NOT equivalent there** (`*a#b,`, checked); the item's count of 74,160 differing strings out of 6,725,601 is its agent's and was NOT re-counted here.

Evidence. **Equivalence**: the oracle files enumerate (strings, not the TRUE counts the commit messages quoted for some): html 14,326,611 strings of which 8,629,722 are TRUE for the old function, link 18,401,487, knitr 18,670,866 (11,619,477 names found), yaml 16,261,697 (all pinned in the tests as `compared` and `hasText`/`junk`/`names`/`matched`, each from the old pattern and the reading by definition and never from the code under test), plus 300,000 seeded texts and a grid of runs each; a scratch differential outside the suite compared 216,493,741 strings (html 48.4M, link 72.6M, knitr 47.1M, yaml 48.4M), 0 differences, positive controls reaching both answers. **Timing**: every shape linear to 1,000,000 characters; no constant-factor regression on a shape the parent read linearly beyond what the review round measured and decided (below). **Rows**: the "Session 334" describe of `pathological-inputs.test.ts` holds 32 document rows (10 controls that pass on the parent, 22 that fail on it on the clock alone, 2.9 to 3.6 s against 1,000 ms; `8cbb2d2` before any cure) and four oracle files (`html-line-text-equivalence`, `link-attribute-junk-equivalence`, `knitr-option-names-equivalence`, `yaml-alias-name-equivalence`; `JS_WHITESPACE` joined `support/regex-equivalence.ts`, proven equal to `\s` over all 65,536 code units). **Mutation**: 95 edits in six lists, first run 82 killed, 9 argued equivalent, 4 contradictions (a mixed `options(digits = 3, foo = 1)` list at the call site, W01 `every` for `some`; a line feed, a vertical tab and U+0085 in the YAML guard's alphabet), killed by the rows of `0c072f6` (86 killed, 9 survive on purpose). **Suite**: 13,071 tests / 105 files at Phase 0, 13,237 after the cures, 13,458 / 109 now. **Real-document score** unchanged by the cures: `head: scored 2438 agree 2423 (99.38%) divergent 15`.

What the item named and this did NOT touch (carried to the plan's g02 notes): four regexes that are superlinear alone but unreachable from document text (`jupyter-kernel.ts:43`, two `#.*$` tails in `yaml-value-check.ts`, `version.ts:29`); they are left alone, and a caller that ever passes them an unsliced line brings them back. Parts (1), (2), (3), (8), (9) were closed in Sessions 333, 330, 319 and 332, so **the item has no open part and is removed from `BACKLOG.md`**. Commits: `08b271f` (the claim) to `5ebc915` for the session proper, the review round below for the rest.

### 2026-10-28 · [BL-Eight more regexes and one paragraph reader that are quadratic on a long line or a long paragraph, none of them in Session 318's diff] Session 332: part (9) closed -- a long paragraph under from: gfm, commonmark or commonmark_x is read in linear time (`x` x 20,000 lines 28 s to 148 ms; an interrupter 8,000 lines down and 8,000 underline-shaped lines 13.4 s to 134 ms; a three-line unit that pushes and pops a column 8.2 s to 155 ms at 4,000 units)

Claim `e2fb887`. Phase 0 picker: the operator chose part (9), the option marked recommended (placed by finishing a feature). Timed first in fresh processes at HEAD `8e03d50` (load 4 to 12): the item's figures held (`x` x 2,000 / 4,000 / 6,000 334 / 1,210 / 2,667 ms; `- item` and `===` x 5,000 2,051; the repeated `from: gfm` / `text` / `<?php` block 2,577 at 10,000; `~ a` x 1,500 / 3,000 under `commonmark_x` 212 / 763), the controls were linear (`format: gfm` and `from: markdown` 36 ms at 8,000), and a profile put the time on the `titleLineCount` line and the interrupt regexes before the cure was read. The cause is the item's hypothesis: `computeRegions` asked, for EVERY line, whether any line of the paragraph below its first interrupts it (`bodyLines.slice(-wholeParagraph).slice(1).some(...)`, two copies of the paragraph and a fresh `[0, ...columns]` per test) and read the answer only at an underline.

The first versions were wrong in two ways that the review round found and that I reproduced before changing anything: the scan kept its ANSWER under one `ColumnStack.columns()` array and restarted whenever the array changed, and the array is rebuilt at every change of the set of open columns, so a paragraph whose last lines push and pop a column between two underlines was quadratic (`x` x N, `<span>a</span>`, then N units of `===`, `  2. a`, `    - b`: 189 / 621 / 2,298 ms at 1,000 / 2,000 / 4,000 units, 3.3x and 3.7x per doubling, 8.2 s on the parent at 4,000); and the gate gave a line that cannot underline the whole paragraph as its title count, which nothing reads but which made `setextTitle` the paragraph's first line for every later line, so a first line of 20,000 quote markers cost 484 / 951 ms at 2,000 / 4,000 lines against 36 / 55 ms on the parent (17x). The final scan keeps the distinct INDENT columns of the lines it has read that match the interrupt list (a line interrupts when it matches AND is not indented code, and code depends on the open columns), reads each line of a paragraph once and answers any columns from the indents; the else-branch is the default count.

After, fresh process, load 3 to 5 (parent in brackets): `x` x 5,000 53 ms (1.84 s), 20,000 148 ms (28 s); an interrupter 8,000 lines down and 8,000 `===` 134 ms (13.4 s), 32,000 of each 420 ms (226 s); the three-line unit 155 ms at 4,000 and 483 ms at 16,000 (8.2 s; 133 s); a 20,000-marker first line 48 ms at 4,000 lines (55 ms), and 83 ms at 8,000 with no interrupter (6.3 s); two lines that alternate the open columns 214 ms at 8,000 (38.6 s); the repeated block 162 ms at 10,000 (2.2 s); `- item` and `===` 55 ms (1.94 s); `~ a` 57 ms (1.93 s). ### 2026-10-27 · [BL-More quadratic and stack-overflow shapes found by Session 319's sweep and review, none of them in its diff] Session 331: g02 fifteenth session -- part (1), consecutive container-column lines, is linear: the stack of container columns is a `ColumnStack` (16,000 lines 3.9 to 4.1 s before, 55 to 65 ms after)

Part (1) of the item. Claim `673072d`; the operator chose it at the Phase 0 picker (placed by finishing a feature: g02 is in flight and the item says a long definition list is a real document). Strict TDD.

**Measured first, before any code was read** (fresh process, cold, `findHeadings`, load 7 to 10): 16,000 lines of each of `: a`, `~ a`, `  : a`, `> : a`, `[^1]: a`, `A. a`, `I. a`, `aa. a` took 3.9 to 4.1 s (4x per doubling); alternating kinds and indents were quadratic too; a blank line between the lines 92 ms, `- a` 76 ms; `findAllCells`, `indexLabels` and `buildOutline` pay the same (1.1 s at 8,000 lines). Under `from: commonmark_x`, `~ a` x 250 / 500 / 1,000 / 2,000 took 52 / 232 / 1,556 / 11,864 ms (8x per doubling) and plain `a` lines 373 / 1,382 / 5,406 / 20,967 ms at 2,000 / 4,000 / 8,000 / 16,000 with an EMPTY stack: the cubic is the paragraph scan (the S318 item's part (9)) times the stack depth.

**Cause (profile, `node --cpu-prof`, load 7 to 8):** `computeRegions` self 40.7%, `scanRegions` 27.9%, the `.every` and `.some` callbacks 10.6%, `indentedCodeLine` 5.1%, `blockQuoteContentStart` 4.0%; under `commonmark_x` 53% in the paragraph-interrupt callback and 37% in `computeRegions`. The item's hypothesis held: a line that opens a container column and pops nothing (a definition marker, a footnote definition, the list markers the model over-accepts) leaves one more entry, and the dozen readers a line asks (`columnKinds.includes`, `.some` over the parents, `.every` over the deeper entries, `indentedCodeLine`, the `[0, ...contentColumns]` spreads, `blockQuoteContentStart`, a count) scan all of them.

**The cure.** `src/core/qmd/column-stack.ts` (`c2a8bd2`): `ColumnStack` keeps the entries and their order exactly (a pop takes the top entry and stops at the first one not deeper than the line, so [4, 6, 4] differs from [4, 4, 6]) and adds prefix aggregates (the deepest definition column, the latest CommonMark entry), a count of list entries by the column of the entry below them, counts by column, and a cached array of the DISTINCT columns, rebuilt (never edited) only when a column enters or leaves the set. `computeRegions` wires it in (`b65afb0`): a quote swaps the whole stack object at entry and back at exit, `hostColumn` reads `topColumn`, `rawMarkerColumns` stays a call that reads the live stack (it was an alias of the live array), and three consts needed a type annotation (TS7022). The 25 helpers that took the arrays were classified from their code by two blind readers and agreed (all set-only); an independent check ran the whole suite (12,583 of 12,584, the one a load timeout, which passes alone) and 20,000 generated documents with every helper handed a deduplicated, reversed array, with a control that drops the largest column and shows 2,338 differences.

**Tests.** `2bd08b7`: a "Session 331" describe in `test/unit/pathological-inputs.test.ts` (4 controls, 8 shapes at 14,000 lines, 5 indented-heading tails, 4 mixed-kind rows, a cells and a labels entry, and a relative `commonmark_x` row): 20 rows RED on the parent on the clock alone (2.5 to 4.0 s against 1,000 ms, answers already right), 18 to 38 ms after the cure. `c2a8bd2`: `test/unit/column-stack.test.ts` holds the class against the plain arrays and the old expressions (every sequence of up to four operations over 17, 83,521 of them, and 15,000 seeded sequences). `4be6b20` and `8b29b45`: `test/unit/column-stack-wiring.test.ts`, documents with the PARENT's answers for the readers of the wiring that no older row told apart from a wrong answer; they pass on arrival and are RED against the mutants (disclosed). `tools/mutation-run/mutants/s331-column-stack.json`: 80 edits; the first whole-suite run left 12 alive (68 killed, 11 contradicting their expectation), 7 of the 12 killed by new rows, C16, W26 and W30 argued equivalent, W01 and W42 without a witness until the review (below): 77 killed, 3 survive.

**Equivalence.** A parent-versus-cure differential over 600,000 generated documents (definition and list markers mixed with the lines that read the stack) found 0 differences. Its controls showed the limit of such a zero: of 9 deliberate breakages it found 6 and missed M1 (the marker columns read at the line start), M3 (`hostColumn` of 0) and M7 (counting distinct columns); aimed searches then found witnesses for M3 and M7 (`- ::: {.callout-note}` / blank / `  > T` / `  ===`, and two lines of `A. a` before a callout under `commonmark_x`), both pinned, and the review found one for M1.

**After** (quiet machine, load 3, fresh process each, `findHeadings`): 16,000 / 32,000 / 64,000 lines of `: a` 56 / 86 / 144 ms, `~ a` 55 / 87 / 153, `  : a` 65 / 96 / 165, `> : a` 61 / 100 / 154, `[^1]: a` 56 / 86 / 145, `A. a` 63 / 97 / 167, `I. a` 62 / 96 / 168, `aa. a` 63 / 96 / 165; `: a` x 16,000 / 64,000 through `findAllCells` 55 / 143, `indexLabels` 61 / 160, `buildOutline` 57 / 147. Under `commonmark_x`, `: a` x 2,000 / 16,000 / 64,000 32 / 140 / 441 ms (the parent 143 and 4,530 at the first two), `~ a` x 1,500 / 3,000 / 6,000 203 / 744 / 2,842 ms against 189 / 682 / 2,603 for plain `a` lines (the paragraph scan, part (9), is now the whole cost). 250 and 750 nested bullets then 40,000 `: a` lines 317 and 799 ms (the cost per line is in the DISTINCT columns open).

**Gates.** `npm test` 12,653 passing in 102 files (the baseline was 12,584 in 100), `npm run check-types` and `npm run package` clean (44 files, 1.87 MB), `check-backlog` OK (232 open items), `npm run test:oracle` 2 files pass, the Outline score 2423 of 2438 (99.38%), 15 divergent, unchanged. `test:integration` NOT run (core-only, it takes the screen; nobody was asked).

Phase 0 found no ledger or receipt gap. The operator chose the pair at the Phase 0 picker (recommended; placed by finishing a feature: Sessions 324 and 325 made the rest of the heading scan linear). Timed first, in fresh processes, before any code was read: the item's figures held (`# a ` + `#` x 10,000 / 20,000 / 40,000: 341 / 1,383 / 5,423 ms cold, the item's 320 / 1,273; `{` x 10,000 / 20,000 / 40,000: 58 / 211 / 806 ms, the item's 50 / 190; 64,000 backslashes 2.3 s). `node --cpu-prof` named the cause before the cure was read: 98.5% of the `#` shape in the closing regex, 94.1% of the `{` shape in the attribute regex, 47.5% and 47.3% for a bare backslash run (both regexes: the lookbehind walks the run at every index, with no `#` and no `{` on the line). RED first (`c0647be`): 20 rows in a "Session 330" describe of `pathological-inputs.test.ts`, 7 controls and 13 refused shapes; the 13 fail on the unmodified source on the clock alone (3.2 to 3.9 s at a load of 4 to 6, `expected N to be less than 1000` after the answer assertion passed; a re-run at load 38 to 68 gave 3.4 to 4.1 s). The cure (`72f4762`): `atxClosingPandocStart` (the final run of `#` before the trailing blanks; its first hash when the backslash run before it is even, else its second) and `headingAttributeStart` (the last non-blank `}`, then the first `{` after the `}` before it whose backslash run is even), both on `evenBackslashesBefore`, and `buildHeading` cutting at the index and asking for the attribute once (it ran the regex twice). ### 2026-10-27 · [BL-What is still quadratic or deep enough to overflow after Session 311, every shape re-measured on the final tree (`066cfcc1`) and none of it in that session's diff] Session 328: part (3) closed -- refused fence openers with no blank line between them are read once per kind of opener, not once per opener

g02's twelfth session (the operator chose part (3) at the Phase 0 picker, the option marked recommended, placed by finishing a feature: Session 327 closed part (6) of the same item; claim `fdd1f66`, 2026-10-27). An opener whose info string pandoc refuses, or a tilde fence under an open paragraph, is read as text and swallows the closer below it into an inline code span; `consumedCloserLine` found that closer by walking every non-blank line below the opener to a blank line or a closer, so N refused openers in one run were N(N + 1)/2 tests of `isCloser`.

### 2026-10-19 · [BL-Nine more single-line overflow and slow-input families, none of them in Session 317's diff] Session 324: part (1) closed -- `ATTR_ATOM_RUN` is the atom grammar factored (dashes, at most one class, then ids), not an alternation of atoms under a `+`, so a refused token costs one pass

g02's eighth session (operator chose it at the Phase 0 picker; claim `d9be8ec`, 2026-10-19). The item's exponential held and was wider than it said: `ATTR_ATOM_RUN` read `-`, `#id` and `.class` atoms under a `+`, and an id's tail and a class's tail both hold `.` and `-`, so `.A.A.A` was one class or three and a token whose last character is wrong (`!`) was tried under every split. It was also QUADRATIC for a run of dashes after an id or a class (the end of the id can fall at any dash), and every route that judges an attribute block reaches it: a heading, a backtick and a tilde fence info string, an image, a span, a div (seven routes, each timed). The cure is the same language written as its shape, `ATTR_ATOM_RUN = /^(?!$)-*(?:\.\p{L}[\p{L}\p{N}_:.-]*)?(?:#[\p{L}\p{N}_:.-]+)*$/u` (exported, like `THEMATIC_BREAK` and `FENCE_OPEN`, so the old spelling can be compared): before the first `#` only dashes and ONE class can stand (a class's tail takes every identifier character after it), and each `#` opens an id that takes everything up to the next `#`.

- **Measured, mine (`findHeadings`, one fresh process each, COLD unless it says warm; the load is beside each):** the parent 276 / 1,094 / 4,326 / 17,230 ms at 22 / 24 / 26 / 28 classes (load 5; 4 times per two classes; the item's cold figures were 285 / 1,138 / 4.5 s), warm 30 / 115 / 455 ms at 22 / 24 / 26 (about 10 times faster: the first run of a pattern in a process is interpreted); an id before the classes 137 / 539 / 2,187 / 8,649 ms at 20 / 22 / 24 / 26; `.A-` repeated 38 / 312 / 2,764 ms at 12 / 14 / 16 (three splits a boundary); an id or a class then dashes 82 / 317 / 1,259 ms at 8,000 / 16,000 / 32,000. The cure 2.6 to 2.9 ms at 22 to 30 classes (load 18), 15 ms at 100,000 classes, 10 ms at 100,000 dashes after an id (the parent 12.3 s in the RED run, load 6 to 8). The item's "74-byte line" was never re-run: the row timed is 68 bytes (`# H {` + 30 x `.A` + `!}`), 7.4 s warm on the parent.
- **Commits, RED first:** `047b5fa` 19 document rows in `pathological-inputs.test.ts` (7 controls pass on the parent, 12 refused-shape rows fail on the clock alone at 3.7 to 12.3 s against 1,000 ms, answers already equal; sized from the WARM figures, the controls first so the pattern is warm); `8432555` the cure and its docblock (`src/core/qmd/model.ts` only; the 12 rows then take 0 to 5 ms); `15a8a04` `attr-atom-run-equivalence.test.ts` (79 tests at the commit; the old spelling kept verbatim as the oracle); `2d717b2` 43 mutants and a README row; `8b57d78` the review round's rows, 5 mutants and the README; `9677869` the review round's comment fixes. The equivalence file passes on arrival (old compared with new): its RED is a mutant, shown below. The `15a8a04` message says "55 hand-stated tokens" (there are 56); the `8432555` message says "a 74-byte heading" (68); the `2d717b2` message says 43 mutants (the list grew to 50 in `8b57d78`). History is not rewritten.
- **Proof:** every string of up to 6 / 9 / 6 characters over three alphabets (299,593 + 2,441,406 + 299,593; accepted 18,983 / 68,741 / 21,694, counted by a memoised parser that uses no regular expression, `tools/attr-atom-count/count_accepted.py`, committed in the close-out, and equal to the JS count of the old pattern), 100,000 seeded tokens (25,927 accepted, counted by a Python port of the generator, equal), every BMP code unit alone and in five places (393,216 texts; the accepted count is NOT pinned, it depends on the Unicode version: 198,143 under Node 22.23 / Unicode 17, 197,855 by Python's Unicode 13), 56 hand-stated tokens each asserted on the old pattern, a wrong pattern shown reported, 21 near-miss shapes timed at 20,000 / 50,000 / 1,000,000 characters (0 to 5 ms a shape against a limit of 100 ms), and a row that 9,000,000 dashes are accepted (the old alternation answered 8,000,000 and threw `RangeError` at 9,000,000: a gain of the cure, pinned). The equivalence file runs 85 tests in under a second.
- **Mutation (`s317-attr-atom-run.json`, the runner alone, `-t 'Session 324'`):** 50 edits, 46 killed and 4 argued equivalent (E01 a lookahead for the empty-token guard, E02 lazy leading dashes, E03 `\p{Letter}`, E04 `{0,}`; the mutation lens independently found no string that tells them from the base). ### 2026-10-19 · [BL-Other regexes still quadratic on a long run of blanks, CR or U+2028 after Session 312: a sweep's list, the code-cell header tail first] Session 323: part (6a) closed -- the nested-value enumerator's ancestor climb builds each container's chain once and keeps it in the index, not a climb of every ancestor for every line

g02's seventh session (operator chose it at the Phase 0 picker; claim `236624d`, 2026-10-18). The item's cause held and it was ONE function for all four entries: `findNestedFrontMatterValueLines` asks `nestedParentPath` for the container of every nested line, and with an index it still climbed every ancestor, reading each one's whole indent twice (`leadingWsLen` `^[ \t]*` and `mappingContainerKey` `^[ \t]+`, 36% and 36% of a profile at 1,600 levels, 9% in the function itself) and `unshift`ing its key: a line `d` levels down cost `d` hops of `d` characters, cubic in the depth while the text grows with its square. Given an index, `containerChain` builds each content line's chain of containers once from the chain of its nearest shallower line and keeps it in `index.chains` (a linked list `{key, above, depth, root}`, so the chains of a document share their tails; `null` for a failed climb; the lines waiting are two arrays, not the stack, because 12,000 levels is a size a pasted file can reach and a recursive spelling throws a `RangeError` there); `containerKeys` writes a fresh path array for each answer, because callers keep it. The rules are the old function's: a column-0 parent answers `[key]` only if the key is in `NESTED_CONTAINERS`, a deeper parent answers the path only if the column-0 root is `format`. The unindexed branch (`nestedKeyContextAt`, the completion path, one question at a cursor) is byte-for-byte the old climb.

- **Measured, mine (`findNestedFrontMatterValueLines`, one fresh process each, two runs, one key per level, load 8 to 10; the text grows 3.9 to 4.0 times per doubling):** the parent 27 / 57 / 252 / 1,512 to 1,585 / 10,485 to 10,791 ms at 200 / 400 / 800 / 1,600 / 3,200 levels (ratios 2.1, 4.4, 6.0, 6.9), the fix 19 / 27 / 54 / 142 / 498 to 522 ms (1.4, 2.0, 2.6, 3.5); 800 levels and 8,000 siblings (6.8 MB) 6.3 to 7.0 s before and 0.81 to 0.83 s after; `resolveDocumentEngine` 606 ms and `collectValueSources` 581 ms at 3,200 levels after (they call it). At load about 40, before any code was read, the item's numbers held: 22 / 48 / 202 / 1,203 / 8,262 ms, 5.2 s for the trunk and siblings. Three sets of runs, three loads: the review's records and performance lenses read 8,324 to 8,410 ms before and 400 to 409 ms after at 3,200 levels (load 4.7 to 19). What is left of the per-byte cost (75 to 90 ns) is the region scanners' linear passes; `containerChain` and `containerKeys` are 4.6% of a profile.

### 2026-10-18 · [BL-Other regexes still quadratic on a long run of blanks, CR or U+2028 after Session 312: a sweep's list, the code-cell header tail first] Session 321: part (3) closed -- the gap between `#|` and a cell option key is read by a scan back from the end, not an unanchored search

g02's fifth session (operator chose it at the Phase 0 picker; claim `c956feb5`). The item named one site and it was one site: `indexLabels` read the blanks between a cell option line's pipe and its key with `/[ \t]*$/.exec(text)?.[0] ?? ""` over the text before the key. That search is retried from every position, so a blank run that is not at the end of that text (the run between the `#` and the `|`) was scanned to its end from each of its blanks and then refused. `trailingBlanks` (`refs.ts`, exported) takes the run from `trimEndBlanks`, which scans back from the end. `#` plus n blanks plus `| label: lst-a` through bare `indexLabels`, fresh processes at load about 5: 42 / 156 / 614 ms at 8,000 / 16,000 / 32,000 blanks and 5.98 s at 100,000 before; 3.6 ms, 3.8 ms and 8.2 ms at 32,000, 100,000 and 1,000,000 after.

- **Commits, RED first:** `f36f6634` the helper and its call, 7 document rows in `pathological-inputs.test.ts` (6 fail on the parent at 6.0 to 6.8 s against a 1 s limit; the control, where the run is the gap itself, passes) and `cell-option-gap-equivalence.test.ts` (the old spelling kept as the oracle); `c38230fb` 14 mutants, the README row and 7 behaviour rows (a list-item cell and a CRLF document). Disclosed: the helper-level rows could only be RED as a missing export, and the behaviour rows pass before and after (pins); the 6 document rows and the near-miss clock rows fail for the right reason, time.
- **Proof:** the old spelling against `trailingBlanks` on every string of three alphabets (488,281 / 137,257 / 299,593 strings; counts of the old answers also taken with Python's `re`), 100,000 seeded texts, and near-miss clock rows; a parent-versus-fix differential over 800,000 generated documents found no difference (its first-run, spaces-only and off-by-one controls were visible; the NBSP control was not, because the call site only hands the helper ASCII blanks: the helper-level rows pin that).
- **Mutation:** `b89-cell-option-gap.json`, 14 edits (the old search back, one character too few or too many, `trimEnd()`, spaces only, the answer inverted, the first run anywhere, caps of 1 and 4,096, five edits of the call), all killed; C13 (the raw line, indent not taken off) died only on the list-item rows added after the first draft.
- **Cost, accepted:** the scan back is about 4 times the old search when the gap itself ends in a very long run (1,000,000 blanks: 3.6 ms against 0.7; 50,000,000: 140 ms against 35), the trade `trimEndBlanks` already carries.
- **Gates:** `npm test` 11,844 passing in 91 files (baseline 11,776 in 90), `npm run check-types` clean, `test:oracle` 2 of 2, `npm run package` 44 files (1.86 MB), `check-backlog` OK (224 open items), the real-document score 2423 of 2438 (99.38%, the same divergent lists), the plan's key count 221.

g02's fourth session (operator chose it at the Phase 0 picker; claim `9d0ffbcb`). The item's list was a sample again: five sites. `TABLE_RULE_ROW` held two quadratics that need no terminator (the pipe body's last cell, an optional pipe and the shared `\s*` touch; the grid body's `[-+=: ]*` shares the space with `\s*`; a three-blank indent made it 4 times worse): the pipe body takes `PIPE_DELIMITER_ROW`'s spelling and the grid body is `\+(?:[-+=: ]*[-+=:])?`. `ATX_CLOSING` was an unanchored search that began with `[ \t]+` and was tried from every blank of a run (`x`, 32,000 blanks, `y`: 620 ms): `(?<![ \t])` makes a run's first blank the only start, exact because the leftmost match never starts mid-run (the index is compared too). The two `[ \t]+#.*$` strips of `knitrDocumentDefaults` are `cutHashComment(…, " \t").trim()` (quadratic on a plain blank run, on trailing blanks, on one hash and a terminator, and on ` #` pairs). `parseOutputPath` reads the marker's line once (`Output created:([ \t]*)(.*)`) and cuts the trailing blanks by a scan, keeping the lazy pattern's odd answers; its quadratic is real and unlikely (about 7,000 consecutive blanks on an `Output created:` line to cost 100 ms).

### 2026-10-16 · [BL-What is still quadratic or deep enough to overflow after Session 311, every shape re-measured on the final tree (`066cfcc1`) and none of it in that session's diff] Session 318: part (7) closed -- three patterns, not the `\s*$` tails the item named

`FENCE_OPEN`, `INDENTED_CELL_FENCE_OPEN` and `DIV_FENCE` read a run of fence characters and then `(.*)$`; a CR, U+2028 or U+2029 makes the tail fail, and the engine retried it once per split of the run (one `exec` of the old spelling: 0.6 s at 32,000 backticks, 6 s at 100,000; a document 12 s, because it reads the line twice). Each run now ends in a negative lookahead, so a shorter run is refused at once, and a match is unchanged because the longest try is the only one that can succeed. The closer `\s*$` tails the item suspected are linear (measured). `BACKLOG.md` marks part (7) CLOSED; the `:::` opener's blank tail stays with B89 part (5).

The item named `indexLabels`; the throw is in `scanRegions`, so every reader answered nothing. The four fence patterns lose their quantified back-reference (same strings, same groups: 3,843,200 comparisons, 0 differ; no overflow to 100 million characters), and `buildCloserIndex` files closer runs over 64 on a side list instead of one bucket per length (5,000,000 buckets, 3.6 s and an exhaustible heap). The 5 MB document reads in 311 ms (a `RangeError` at the parent). 200,000 generated fence documents equal the parent. The 5 MB row is RED first; the ten pin rows passed on arrival (the message says eleven; ten). The side list had a regression, fixed in `066cfcc1`.

### 2026-10-15 · [BL-Three PRE-EXISTING slow or crashing inputs, found by Session 304's robustness hunter and timed firsthand — identical in the parent build] `8e17c0f2` fix(robustness): an unclosed front-matter flow mapping is read in time proportional to the block

`flowRegion` re-read the whole joined text after every added line: 20,000 lines under an unclosed `format: {` cost 26 s in every reader of the front matter (the item could not locate the reader; one profile did). `flowEntries` wraps `flowScan`, which also reports the closing bracket, and `flowRegion` reads windows of 1, 2, 4, 8 lines. 140,000 generated front matters equal the parent. Two rows RED first (5.4 and 5.6 s, now about 100 ms). Residual, disclosed and now filed: 5,000 repeated `format: {` openers take 5.3 s (cubic at the parent).

### 2026-10-15 · [BL-Three PRE-EXISTING slow or crashing inputs, found by Session 304's robustness hunter and timed firsthand — identical in the parent build] `52e5c4ee` fix(robustness): an out-of-range numeric character reference in an id no longer throws the label index

`writtenElsewhere` decoded `&#N;` with `String.fromCodePoint`, which throws above U+10FFFF and took the whole index down for a document with a labelled cell the no-target rules would remove. Such a reference is now U+FFFD, which the identifier filter drops. Three rows RED first (the `RangeError`); two pin rows added in `066cfcc1` show the decode is what is described.

### 2026-10-07 · [BL-CommonMark-family readers: what the Session 296 rules still miss — the container model, the quote extent, raw HTML inside a container and a div that interrupts a paragraph; all measured, corpus reach 0 of 3,383] Session 297: the four run-to-the-end rules of the CommonMark readers fire only where the front matter PROVES the reader

`src/core/qmd/model.ts` (`1d237399` claim; checkpoints `33b562f6`, `2c9211f4`, `c4952c91`, `6f38b80a`, `b6091c80`, `0c711ec7`; rows `7c582732`): the backlog's row (6) said the front-matter `from:` resolver reads a CommonMark reader where quarto reads the default for six rendered shapes (a `from:` inside a flow collection, a re-declared anchor, a block inside a fence), and Session 296's run-to-the-end rules then delete every heading below a trigger; the fix was priced as "a plain top-level scalar test". The six controls reproduced (seven: control 046 lost more headings too), and were a sample of an open-ended space. Measured first: the controls' cached renders, a 100-spelling x 5-trigger grid (500 documents, `scratchpad/s297/gf`: the parent deleted in 55 cells) and a sixth trigger (an unclosed `<!--`, `gf2`). Result: `frontMatterProvesReader` and a `commonmarkProven` flag gating FOUR sites: the unclosed plain fence, the type 3-5 HTML block, the degenerate comment (Session 296) and the unclosed-comment swallow (Session 289, found by the sixth trigger: the identical hole in the identical 11 spellings, in the pre-Session-296 build too). The proof is a WHITELIST: the front matter opens after ASCII blank lines only with a non-blank first line, quarto's own region scanner agrees on that one region, no lone `\r`, no fence marker, `<!--` or line starting with `---` inside it, no include shortcode or `metadata-file(s)` key anywhere, a body that holds a `---` delimiter (also one an HTML comment disguises) mentions no `from`, backslash or `<!--`, every front-matter line fits a small grammar (plain keys and list items whose values close on their line, block text skipped by indent, wrapped quotes and flows followed only from a value start, a plain scalar's continuation line declines), and exactly one governing `from` exists (column 0, or under `format:` / `html:` as the only format with no body delimiter) whose text is byte-identical to the resolver's. Three designs, each broken by blind authors and each time by a new CLASS: a bracket counter (round 1: 36 documents the pre-Session-296 build had right), a quote lexer (round 2: a glued `#`, a colon glued to a quote, odd dash-item keys, non-ASCII whitespace, a lone `\r`, a body block the region scanner's fence tracker hides) and the whitelist (round 3: a comment disguising a delimiter, two blank lines then `----`, a later block replacing `format:`, an include shortcode, `metadata-files`). Measured against quarto and against three critic leads (refuted: `pandoc-args -f markdown`, `--from=`, `--reader=` and a project `_quarto.yml` reader do NOT override a document's own `from:`). Final: 0 of 887 scored blind and realistic documents (19 lens directories) where the pre-Session-296 build is right and this one deletes (the parent: 205; 18 documents delete identically in every build); the grid's deletion cells 55 to 0 and the comment grid's 11 to 0; Session 296's 3,979 probes: 8 lost fixes (controls corpus) and 5 deletions removed, and the 38 regressions against the pre-Session-296 build are all among Session 296's 43 pinned ones; 1,055 misread twins (`pg`): 0 deletions where the older build was right (the Session 296 rules not gated cost 105 phantoms); 334 realistic documents keep 88 of 103 fixes (85%); a differential fuzz against js-yaml (about 5 million documents, 1.25 million on the final build) reports 0 hazards and finds 4,830 in the parent and 963 in the first whitelist; corpus 0 of 3,383 changed; real-document score unchanged at 2,423 of 2,438; 4,463 tests to 4,583 (the Session 297 suite holds 126, six Session 296 rows that pinned the wrong answers retired; 10 RESIDUAL rows pin phantoms the fix leaves). Mutation: 93 single-term mutants, 41 killed by the unit suite, 14 more by new rows (11 from their shortest rendered killer documents, 3 by crafted rendered witnesses for guards no document reached), 14 left because the mutant is RIGHT where the real build declines on purpose, 24 unobservable (invalid-YAML shapes or subsumed guards). Residuals and every decline are filed in the backlog (a new item, with the measured costs); the Session 296 rules not gated (105 phantoms, 0 deletions), the critic's five unrendered rule-side leads and the unmeasured knitr and julia engines too; row (6) removed from the CommonMark item; learnings 655-664. ### 2026-10-05 · [BL-The callout rule's "the first block is a heading" test never checks that the heading is a DIRECT child of the div — a heading inside a block quote or list item that opens the callout is suppressed] Session 291: a heading inside a quote or list item that opens a callout is no longer suppressed by the Outline

`src/core/qmd/model.ts` (`calloutTitleTakes`, the setext call site, the first-block marker, the div entry): a callout's first heading is its title bar only when it is the div's first BLOCK and a DIRECT child. Decided by (1) STRICTLY MORE `>` markers than the opener's effective depth, judged at the ATX line or the setext UNDERLINE (fewer or none is a lazy continuation, still lifted; a lazy opener sits at the depth of the quote it continues, carried through unmarked lines; an unmarked underline indented past the host's content column in an UNQUOTED callout hands the decision to the title line's own depth); (2) a setext title whose list marker `setextTitleText` strips (a bullet, or an ordered marker over an underline at the content column) is a heading inside a list item; (3) a bare `>` deeper than the opener is an empty quote and so the first block, recorded in a new `firstBlockLine` so the tabset rule keeps `firstContentLine`. **The filed item was half right:** its LIST half was not the callout rule's at all — an ATX heading on a list-marker line is dropped everywhere (the Session 261 marker-line item), which a plain-div twin showed in minutes (`twin.cjs` + `cmp.cjs`, ATTR CALLOUT-ONLY / BOTH / TWIN-ONLY); the list shapes the callout rule alone deleted are a setext title on a bullet or content-column ordered marker line. Evidence: workflow 1 (10 agents, ~2.5M tokens, five measuring lenses + five refuters, ~8,000 rendered probes) and workflow 2 (4 blind adversarial lenses, ~1M tokens, ~14,000 probes), every claim re-classified mechanically against HEAD and the final build; ~2,900 callout probes deleting to right. 158 probes of the adversarial grid and 17 of the measuring grid go right to PHANTOM, none right to deleting, and each is a pre-existing defect the old over-broad rule had hidden (the plain-div twin is wrong at HEAD too): the ordered-lookalike marker grammar (`Fig.`, `A.`, `Dr.`, `(ab)`, 142), a callout inside a REMOVED conditional block, a lazy opener after two bare nested markers (2). The change is MONOTONE by construction and proved so without an oracle: 196,156 documents (every scratchpad source plus the 3,383-document corpus), 5,319 changed, 5,397 headings added, 0 dropped. Score unchanged 2,423/2,438; corpus 0 of 3,383 moved. TDD: RED shown for each of 14 slices; 3,634 tests (+44); every term of the rule killed by a mutant after two redundant terms (`inQuote`, an entry-depth guard) were removed. Two defects of MY making surfaced only through independent lenses and are fixed: the first cut copied the tabset rule's `inQuote` + `containerDepth` terms (111 HEAD-right probes became phantoms; the mutation run had shown both terms unpinned), and moving the shared `firstContentLine` for the empty-quote rule stopped tabsets learning their tab level. Disclosed, not fixed (two new BACKLOG items; pinned as `RESIDUAL` rows): div lifetime across a marked blank of fewer markers, reader-dependent lazy continuation under the CommonMark-family readers, the tabset rule's own nested-quote hole, the AST first-block cases (footnote / link-reference definitions, YAML above the heading, theorem-like divs). ### 2026-10-05 · [BL-The class-ORDER rule compares only callout-vs-tabset, but quarto dispatches a div to the FIRST class that has ANY registered handler — so `.content-visible` written first wins, and this model still suppresses] Session 290: a conditional class written before a callout/tabset class takes the div dispatch, so the Outline no longer deletes its headings

`src/core/qmd/model.ts` (`5ca18838`): quarto dispatches a div to the FIRST class token that names a registered handler (the callout family, `.panel-tabset`, or `.content-visible` / `.content-hidden`), and the model compared callout-vs-tabset only, so a conditional written first lost the callout's first heading and every tab heading (a deletion). `divHandlerOf` / `firstDivHandler` decide the structured path; the regex fallback gains `conditionalClassIndex` and reads every class from the quote-masked spec (`legacyDivPanelTabset` had read the unmasked one, so a quoted value holding ` .panel-tabset ` made a plain div a tabset; 27 probes deleting→right as a by-catch). Evidence: 1,923 measurement probes in six lenses (only nine class NAMES ever change a dispatch; 0 of 3,383 corpus documents write any class before a callout/tabset) and 3,679 adversarial probes in four lenses, each claim re-rendered by a refuter. TDD: 71 tests, 31 RED at HEAD, RED shown per slice; 12 mutants killed (one survived round one: fused-value guards added). A refuter found the first cut deleted one MORE heading than HEAD in a contrived `commonmark_x` shape; fixed. Re-classifying 4,366 rendered probes against HEAD, the first cut and the final build: 1,292 deleting→right, 107 deleting→phantom, 97 right→phantom (63 where the condition REMOVES the block, 21 theorem/proof, 13 near-miss spellings), 0 right→deleting, 0 deleting more than HEAD. Score unchanged 2,423/2,438; corpus 0 of 3,383 moved; 3,590 tests, types clean. Disclosed, not fixed: removal is not modelled (a phantom; `BACKLOG.md`'s format-gated item records the position taken), a dot-fused class under `commonmark_x` (pinned), and the regex-route residuals (one new BACKLOG item). Commits: claim `b8effaa1`, `5ca18838`, close-out. BACKLOG item removed.

`findHeadings` (`src/core/qmd/model.ts`, `f4939251`): a `<!--` with no `-->` on any line below is paragraph text under pandoc's markdown readers (rendered, 55 probes, both modes), so it falls through as a body line; `gfm`/`commonmark`/`commonmark_x` keep swallowing to EOF (CommonMark HTML block). TDD, RED shown; 48 pinned rows; three mutants killed. Score unchanged 2,423/2,438; 0 of 3,383 corpus documents move. ### 2026-09-20 · [BL-The Outline's last real-document divergences — eight root causes behind the 20 authored documents that still differ from quarto, no trigger of them described by an open item] R5 — the model learns that knitr runs before pandoc, and stops letting a claimed chunk's closer swallow the heading below it
- **Change:** `src/core/qmd/model.ts` — a new `KNITR_CHUNK_INFO` predicate beside `CELL_INFO` (which is UNTOUCHED), a type-only `DocumentEngine` import and an `EngineHint` alias, `scanRegions`/`computeRegions`/`findHeadings` threading an optional engine, `regionsCache` keyed on text **and** engine, and one `knitrClaims` clause at the `refused` computation. `test/unit/qmd-model.test.ts` — the R5 suite: one RED-first behaviour test plus seven guards.
- **Commit/PR:** this entry's own commit
- **Session:** S273 · **Verified:** strict TDD — the behaviour test was written first and shown failing FOR THE RIGHT REASON (`expected [] to deeply equal [ 'After Chunk' ]`, the heading swallowed by the runaway region) before any `src/` edit. `npm run compile` exit 0; `npm test` **66 files / 2971 passed** (2963 at baseline, +8 new, **0 moved**). ⚠ **This commit is behaviour-INERT for every existing caller and that is measured, not asserted:** `tools/realdoc-score/run.sh s273-c1` → `scored 2438 agree 2421 (99.30%) divergent 17 phantom 2 lost 8 level 7`, the byte-identical baseline, because the census is still engine-blind. Every expectation in the suite was measured against real `quarto render` with real knitr execution (quarto 1.7.33, knitr 1.51, R 4.6.1) BEFORE it was written (Learning #552).
- **⚠ The naive predicate was wrong and an adversarial pass caught it.** knitr's own grammar is `[a-zA-Z0-9_]+`, but its rewrite target is a pandoc CLASS and pandoc refuses a class that does not begin with a letter — so a digit-led or `_`-led engine name runs away anyway, one stage later, and HEAD was already right. Shipping knitr's grammar verbatim would have manufactured 5 phantoms and frozen one into the suite. The predicate is knitr's grammar **∩** pandoc's class grammar, `[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*`, with both measured tables in the docstring. Re-measured firsthand: `quarto pandoc` opens `.zzz9`/`.x2`/`.x_`/`.cpp11`/`.glue_sql` and refuses `.2x`/`._x`/`.99`/`.9zzz`; end to end on `.Rmd`, `<h4>` count 1 for `{zzz9,…}`/`{cpp11,…}`/`{glue_sql,…}` and **0** for `{2x,…}`/`{_zzz,…}`.
- **Model:** Claude Opus 5 (claude-opus-5)

### 2026-09-19 · [BL-The Outline's last real-document divergences — eight root causes behind the 20 authored documents that still differ from quarto, no trigger of them described by an open item] R4, HTML half — a block-level tag anywhere in a heading's content means the line is no heading
- **Change:** `src/core/qmd/model.ts` gains `headingAngleEnd`, an HTML5-tokenizer-shaped reader for the `<` branch of the same scan: comments, CDATA sections and processing instructions consume their interior (an UNTERMINATED one consumes nothing), `<!DOCTYPE` does not, the name ends at the first whitespace / `/` / `>` with every other character part of it, and attributes are scanned QUOTE-AWARE — without a `>` outside quotes there is no tag. Names come from the existing `PANDOC_BLOCK_CLOSE_TAGS` list with two measured deltas: `applet` and `object` also refuse as OPENERS (the only 2 of 16 `PANDOC_EITHER_TAGS` that do), and `</script>` alone does not refuse as a CLOSER. A tenth reader flag gates it on THREE bases — `markdown`, `markdown_mmd`, `markdown_phpextra` — a different set from the TeX half's on the same nine documents.
- **Commit/PR:** this entry's own commit
- **Session:** S269 · **Verified:** strict TDD — RED `R4b` shown first (`### 1. <Section heading>`, the corpus shape at ordinal 3004), then GREEN. `tools/realdoc-score/run.sh s269-r4` = **scored 2438 agree 2421 (99.30%) divergent 17 phantom 2 lost 8 level 7**, against a baseline measured this session of agree 2419 (99.22%) divergent 19 phantom 4: ordinals 1985 and 3004 leave the divergent list and the lost and level lists are unchanged. `npm test` 2951/2951; `npm run check-types` exit 0. Name sets measured one document per name (114 opener probes, 119 closer probes) — a single-document sweep of the same names is unsound and reported `applet`/`object` backwards.
- **Found on the way:** one pinned PRE-EXISTING phantom is recovered by this change — `SETEXT_H1`'s documented raw-HTML residual (`<div>` under a `===`, Session 197's Family A). Re-rendered and the pin rewritten; its raw-TeX twin (`\clearpage`) is class B and is unchanged.
- **Model:** Claude Opus 5 (claude-opus-5)

### 2026-09-19 · [BL-The Outline's last real-document divergences — eight root causes behind the 20 authored documents that still differ from quarto, no trigger of them described by an open item] R4, TeX half — a class-A block macro anywhere in a heading's content means the line is no heading
- **Change:** `src/core/qmd/model.ts` gains `headingContentRefused` — a left-to-right SCAN of the heading's raw content (not a containment test) that skips code spans, `\`-escapes, math, bracketed inline content, quarto shortcodes and a macro's own argument groups, and refuses the line at a class-A block macro. Three new anchored name predicates read the EXISTING `PANDOC_BLOCK_MACROS_*` tables under a different arity rule: no tail rule, BARE kills with or without braces, ARG needs a BRACED group (an optional `[…]` does not satisfy it), and the default is ALLOW — an unknown macro keeps its heading, the opposite of `RAW_TEX_BLOCK_OR_MACRO_LINE`. Gated by a ninth reader flag, `fromRefusesBlockInHeading`, whose fail-safe answer is KEEP and whose `-raw_tex` extension outranks the base name. Read at ONE site, `buildHeading`, so the ATX and setext paths share it.
- **Commit/PR:** this entry's own commit
- **Session:** S269 · **Verified:** strict TDD — RED `R4a` shown first, failing with the phantom `h2:\maketitle` (the corpus shape, ordinal 1985), then GREEN. `npm test` 2950/2950 (66 files); `npm run check-types` exit 0; the 19 `Session 269 GUARD` rows still green. The allow/deny direction is measured: a default-deny predicate reaches 18 corpus documents and 73 heading lines, against the 1 line R4 is about.

### 2026-08-27 · [ad hoc] Backfilled (reconcile-on-read): undocumented commits `9d0aecd4`..`f6c8ee3e` — Session 259 shipped the callout attribute-value fix and then ended without any close-out

- ⚠ **This entry is a Phase 0 reconcile written on 2026-09-09, not a close-out.** Session 259
  claimed the task, authored five commits, and left **no** `CHANGELOG.md` entry, **no** completed
  `HANDOFFS.md` receipt (its 1B stub is still `status: pending`), no `SESSION_NOTES.md` close-out
  pair, no `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` rows, and did not remove the completed item from `BACKLOG.md`.
  The commits are summarised from their own messages and from the artifacts in
  `scratchpad/s259/`; nothing here is a claim this session verified beyond the gates named below.
- **What the commits did — `BACKLOG.md` "Up Next" head item, the HIGH heading-DELETING defect
  filed by Session 258 against Session 257's shipped `divCalloutTitleSlot`.** The predicate tested
  its two class patterns against the WHOLE attribute spec, so any `.callout…` substring anywhere in
  that spec read as a class and deleted the heading below it.
- **The filed item called the fix "one token"; the commit message reports it is three** — a leading
  token boundary `(?:^|[\s{])`, a trailing terminator `(?=[\s}]|#[\w:.-])` (a `.` is an identifier
  character to pandoc, so `{.callout-note.foo}` is ONE class literally named `callout-note.foo`),
  and `maskQuotedValues()`, because a quoted value may hold a token that is whitespace-delimited on
  both sides (`data-x="a .callout-note b"`) that no boundary test can distinguish from a class.
- **A fourth commit reversed a guard's stated reason after mutation refuted it.** Mutant M8 swapped
  the mask's filler for a space and flipped nothing; the probe built to separate the claim showed a
  closing quote OPENS the next token rather than ending one (`::: {.note x="q".callout-note}` is a
  real callout, rendered), so masking the quotes away had introduced a phantom two commits earlier.
  The boundary is asymmetric: a quote may PRECEDE a class, it may not TERMINATE one.
- **Evidence recorded in the commits.** A 43-probe grid rendered through quarto 1.7.33
  (`scratchpad/s259/probes`), both builds run side by side from a worktree at the parent commit:
  baseline **25 divergent (24 deletions + 1 phantom) → 3**. The 3 that remain are Session 258's
  shipped `divPanelTabset` carrying the same two holes (h1/h3/k1) — **and, contrary to the commit
  messages' "filed, not fixed", no `BACKLOG.md` item for them exists; the filing never happened.**
  Strict TDD is claimed per commit with the RED rows named (R1–R10, R3/R5/R7/R9 as controls).
- **Gates re-run at this reconcile, 2026-09-09, on the tree as committed:** `npm run compile`
  clean, `npm test` **2868 passing / 66 files**, `node check-backlog.js` OK (198 open). No runtime
  smoke test (Phase 3E) is recorded by Session 259 and none was run here.
- **Commits (5).** `9d0aecd4` claim · `23231f40` the token boundary, the terminator and the value
  mask (R1–R5) · `bd1382db` the `title=`-inside-a-value read (R6–R7) · `0a6b72a0` the closing-quote
  asymmetry, found by mutation (R8–R10) · `f6c8ee3e` the comment correction, no behaviour change.

### 2026-08-26 · [BACKLOG: EVERY heading inside a `::: {.panel-tabset}` is a TAB LABEL] The panel-tabset tab-label divergence — 21 authored documents fixed, 0 regressions

- **Shipped the rule.** Every heading at a `.panel-tabset` div's TAB LEVEL is a tab label, not a
  heading: quarto's filter puts its inlines in an `<a role="tab">` nav link and emits no `<hN>`.
  `findHeadings` reported all of them, so the Outline offered sections the reader never sees.
- **The rule is quarto's own code, not an inference.** `parse_tabset_contents`
  (`/Applications/quarto/share/filters/main.lua:21643`) takes the FIRST `Header` among the div's
  DIRECT blocks, freezes its level, and makes a tab of every direct-child `Header` at EXACTLY that
  level. Three consequences the filed item did not state, each rendered before any code was written:
  the tab level is the FIRST heading's level and **not** the shallowest (`### First` / `## Second`
  renders `h2:Second`); the test is EQUALITY and not `level <= tabLevel` (a `#` between two `##`
  tabs renders as a real `h1`); and tab-hood is a CONTAINER test, so a heading inside a nested div,
  list item or quote is real at the very level its siblings were consumed at.
- **Measured over Session 251's 3,383-document authored corpus**, baseline regenerated from the
  parent commit in a throwaway worktree: structural divergence **80 → 59 documents**, agreement
  **96.72% → 97.58%**, **21 fixed / 0 regressed**; at heading granularity **151 removed, 0 added,
  and 0 of the removals are headings quarto renders**, with 0 newly-introduced losses across all
  2,438 scored documents.
- ⚠ **My own adversarial pass found ELEVEN category-A defects in code that had already passed a
  90-document rendered grid, 2,844 unit tests and the corpus score.** The shape: S257's callout rule
  claims ONE line, so every pre-existing "the div stack is wrong here" defect cost at most one
  heading — a tabset claims EVERY heading at its tab level for as long as the entry lives, which
  turns each of them into an unbounded deletion. Four fixes, each driven RED first: a div opened
  inside a QUOTE now dies with the quote (it was deleting headings in *later* quotes, three in one
  witness); a div naming BOTH a callout and a tabset is resolved by CLASS ORDER; the tab level is
  learned only from the div's FIRST CONTENT LINE (the fail-safe against a phantom heading, or a real
  `Header` this model cannot see, setting the wrong level); and the six readers without
  `fenced_divs` have no div to suppress.
- **Closed two long-standing pinned residuals as a side effect**, re-rendered to confirm: Session
  229 R5 and Session 233 R1 — a div opened inside a quote and "closed" outside it. Both assertions
  are reversed in place.
- **Evidence.** A 117-document rendered grid (103 agree, **0 lost**, 12 disclosed phantoms, 2 quarto
  errors) and TWELVE mutants that each redden the shipped rows — `scratchpad/s258/mutate.py`,
  `mutate_tests.py`. Runtime gate satisfied: `npm run test:integration` **544 passing, exit 0**,
  including a new `outline.test.ts` row driving `vscode.executeDocumentSymbolProvider`.
- **Commits (6).** `5da87bc5` claim · `80fce2da` the rule, 27 unit rows and the runtime row ·
  `e12cb34b` the four post-adversarial fixes · `d519f434` the records · `3c1f2dda` close-out ·
  plus the both-ledgers commit and a final telemetry append (`.context-budget-history.jsonl`,
  `dashboard_history.jsonl`), folded into the same commit as this line so BOTH frontiers stay at
  `HEAD`. ⚠ **A LEDGER CANNOT RECORD THE COMMIT
  THAT FOLLOWS IT**, so whichever of `CHANGELOG.md` / `HANDOFFS.md` moves last is the only one
  with a zero frontier — touch BOTH in the final commit or the next Phase 0 reconcile chases a
  phantom gap (inherited from Session 257, and it held).
- **Model:** Claude Opus 5.

### 2026-08-26 · [BACKLOG: A heading inside a CALLOUT is consumed as the callout's title] The callout title slot — 44 authored documents fixed, 0 regressions

- **The defect.** Quarto's callout filter lifts a callout div's FIRST BLOCK, when it is a header,
  into the title bar: it emits no `<hN>`, no `<section>` and no `sec-` anchor for it. An `@sec-`
  reference to such a heading renders as quarto's unresolved `<strong>?@sec-hotel</strong>`.
  `findHeadings` reported the heading anyway, putting a section in the Outline the reader never
  sees — and offering a cross-reference target quarto cannot resolve. Filed by Session 205, ranked
  #1 by real-document reach by the Session 251 audit, unfixed for 52 sessions.
- **The measured result, over Session 251's 3,383-document authored corpus.** Structural
  divergence **124 → 80 documents**; structural agreement **94.91% → 96.72%**. **44 documents
  fixed, 0 regressed.** At heading granularity: **138 headings removed, 0 added, and 0 of the
  removals are headings quarto actually renders** — no deletion in the forbidden direction. 29 of
  the 138 sit in 20 documents quarto itself refuses to render (exit ≠ 0), so they are unscorable
  and disclosed rather than counted. Baseline regenerated from the parent commit in a throwaway
  worktree rather than read from S251's stored census, which was several model changes stale.
- **The rule is narrow, and every boundary was rendered before it was coded.** It fires only when
  the heading is the callout's own first block: a paragraph, code block, thematic break, HTML
  comment or nested div above it all defeat the lift, and only the FIRST heading is taken — a
  second one in the same callout is a real heading. It keys on a closed set of types
  (`note`/`tip`/`warning`/`caution`/`important`) plus **bare `.callout`**, which renders
  `callout-none` chrome and lifts a title exactly as the named five do — the one member Quarto's
  own documentation would not predict. `.callout-bogus` and `.note` are not callouts at all.
  ⚠ **A NON-EMPTY `title=` takes the slot and the heading then SURVIVES**, but `title=""` and
  `title=''` do not, and `title="   "` does — the gate is an empty quoted value, not the presence
  of the attribute. That last one was found by an adversarial verifier attacking the first cut of
  the predicate, and re-measured firsthand before it was believed.
- **Disclosed residual, declined on a measurement.** The filed item names two spellings and this
  closes one. The RAW-HTML form (`<div class="callout-note">`) really does render a callout and is
  still a phantom — but **244 of the 3,383 authored documents carry a fenced callout div and ZERO
  carry a raw-HTML one**, and closing it needs a `<div>`/`</div>` container the scanner does not
  have. Re-filed in `BACKLOG.md` with that measurement and pinned live as guard G12, so the
  phantom is asserted rather than forgotten.
- Also closes **two disclosed-residual pins** that Sessions 193 and 215 each filed against this
  exact phantom, and files two new items: the format-gated `.content-hidden` / `.content-visible`
  divs (NEW, unfiled — quarto deletes the whole block, but the answer is target-format-dependent,
  so the decision is the hard part) and the negative result recording the twelve div classes
  measured NOT to consume a heading, `.tabset` among them.
- Strict TDD: R1–R4 each shown RED before GREEN, R4 twice. 19 new assertions plus a drift gate
  asserting `model.ts`'s `CALLOUT_TYPES` equals `notebook-callout.ts`'s `CALLOUT_TITLES` key set.
  `npm test` 2817 / 66 files; `compile` clean; `check-package` OK 42 files / 5.60 MB;
  `check-backlog` OK 195 open.
- **The runtime gate was SATISFIED, not waived** — operator-authorised, since it takes over the
  screen. `npm run test:integration` launched a real VS Code Extension Development Host:
  **543 passing, exit 0**, including a new `outline.test.ts` row that drives
  `vscode.executeDocumentSymbolProvider` — the command the Outline view itself calls — and
  asserts the lifted heading is absent, a SECOND heading in the same callout is present, the
  headings outside it are present, and the same document with a non-callout class keeps its
  heading. Commits (7): `e7dd276e` claim, `a80a6de8` the rule, `ae9e8d94` the empty-title
  correction plus guards and gate, `c89b9608` the runtime test, `27485730` the records

  `docs/archive/SESSION_NOTES-through-S253.md.verify.sh`, C0–C6, modelled on canonical's
  `BACKLOG-archive-2026-08-15.md.verify.sh`. It is keyed on each record's own heading identity,

  identity-modulo-a-transform; stated cost is that relative links inside archived records resolve
  against the repo root.

  injected defect classes (dropped record, edited record, duplicated record, swapped halves,
  false count, tampered front matter). That RED pass found **three real defects in this session's
  own tooling** — an off-by-one trailing newline that gave the last archived record a byte the
  original never had, a proof that crashed instead of reporting FAIL, and a corruption mode that
  never reached the check it targeted. All three fixed, then GREEN: C0–C6 pass, 743 records =
  5 retained + 738 archived, byte accounting exact.
